<<<ADR: ADR-INT-001.md>>>
# ADR-INT-001 — INT's host-facing surface is four write operations — start a Check, hand over a manual upload, confirm the uploads, record the Employee Decision — and every read stays with the module that owns the data; no server-rendered report page is planned
Status      : RESOLVED-IN-DIALOGUE
Stage       : P0        Module: INT        Version: v1
Lane        : analysis-dialogue · operator (single-operator converging dialogue)
Decided     : 2026-10-01T15:00:00+00:00
Dialogue-key: 9a549066d179
traces      : POL-INT-001, POL-INT-004, POL-INT-006, POL-INT-007, POL-INT-016

## Decision
Raw idea §8 lists five endpoints; A1 replaces `GET /checks/{id}/view` with the employee frontend, and the frontend uses the same REST API as any host. RPT already serves the reads of a Check and its report, of the Checks of a request and of the decision agreement over HTTP (ADR-RPT-005, ADR-RPT-006); REG serves its service reads, DOC the list of uploaded documents and CHK the active-Check read. Options: A) INT re-exposes every read as a façade — two paths for one fact, two error catalogs, drift; B) INT owns only what no other module serves over HTTP: the four writes that need orchestration across modules (start → CHK CON-CHK-004; upload → DOC CON-DOC-003; confirm → CHK CON-CHK-005; decision → REG CON-REG-012, host Approval API, RPT CON-RPT-006). Recommended B. The server-rendered page is not planned (A1, domain-profile D6). Status: recommended — confirmed at prd-approval. Sources: [KB:raw-idea.md §8, §11, §15 A1]; domain-profile §6, §8 D6; ADR-RPT-005.

Source      : governance-shared/analysis/modules/INT/_state/briefs/pass-1.md (P0 operator run)

<<<END ADR>>>

<<<ADR: ADR-INT-002.md>>>
# ADR-INT-002 — Starting a Check is accepted asynchronously — INT answers at once with the Check identifier and its first status — and the employee identity the host passes is handed on exactly as sent, with no caller authentication in this version
Status      : RESOLVED-IN-DIALOGUE
Stage       : P0        Module: INT        Version: v1
Lane        : analysis-dialogue · operator (single-operator converging dialogue)
Decided     : 2026-10-01T15:00:00+00:00
Dialogue-key: d717a14bbae3
traces      : POL-INT-001, POL-INT-002

## Decision
Raw idea §5: the host sends the service code, the request number and the employee's identity; a Check takes time, so the host starts it and polls. §8 says the calling system authenticates itself and passes the employee's identity; A2 defers the authentication part. Options for the answer: A) hold the request until the report exists — breaks §5 and the host's request timeout; B) answer as soon as CHK has created the Check run (CON-CHK-004 returns checkId and RUNNING or AWAITING_DOCUMENTS) with the profile's status 202 and a pointer to RPT's read of that Check. Recommended B. Identity: INT passes the employee identity and, at decision time, the deciding employee exactly as the host sent them (profile `conventions.identifiers`); INT neither verifies them against a directory nor adds any caller check — caller authentication returns with the security version (A2, D7). Status: recommended — confirmed at prd-approval. Sources: [KB:raw-idea.md §5, §8, §15 A2]; profile `stack.backend.api.http_statuses` (202); CON-CHK-004.

Source      : governance-shared/analysis/modules/INT/_state/briefs/pass-1.md (P0 operator run)

<<<END ADR>>>

<<<ADR: ADR-INT-003.md>>>
# ADR-INT-003 — A refusal raised by CHK, DOC or RPT reaches the caller in the platform's ProblemDetail form with its owner's code, HTTP status and message unchanged; INT mints INT codes only for what INT itself decides
Status      : RESOLVED-IN-DIALOGUE
Stage       : P0        Module: INT        Version: v1
Lane        : analysis-dialogue · operator (single-operator converging dialogue)
Decided     : 2026-10-01T15:00:00+00:00
Dialogue-key: 74412291c778
traces      : POL-INT-003, POL-INT-005, POL-INT-010

## Decision
The contracts hand INT typed refusals 'for INT to show as a ProblemDetail': CHK's five (start incomplete, service not available, connection not activated, Check not found, Check not waiting for documents — CON-CHK-004, CON-CHK-005), DOC's four upload refusals (CON-DOC-003) and RPT's five decision refusals (CON-RPT-006). Each owner already gives each refusal a code in the profile format `{MOD}-{http}[-{SLUG}]` (ADR-CHK-018, ADR-DOC-012, ADR-RPT-013). Options: A) INT re-codes every refusal as INT-… — a second list that drifts from the owner's; B) INT passes the owner's code, status and message through unchanged and adds INT codes only for its own decisions: a request body it cannot read, an upload to a Check not waiting for documents, an upload above the transport limit, the Approval API failing (502) or not answering in time (504), and an unexpected server failure. Recommended B. Status: recommended — confirmed at prd-approval. Sources: profile `stack.backend.api.error_envelope`, `error_code_format`, `http_statuses`; CON-CHK-004, CON-CHK-005, CON-DOC-003, CON-RPT-006; ADR-RPT-013.

Source      : governance-shared/analysis/modules/INT/_state/briefs/pass-1.md (P0 operator run)

<<<END ADR>>>

<<<ADR: ADR-INT-004.md>>>
# ADR-INT-004 — An Employee Decision is executed through the Approval API only when it is APPROVED, on a COMPLETED Check with no decision yet, and the Check's service package version enables the Approval API; INT calls it once, before recording, and a failed or timed-out call records nothing so the employee can retry; a REJECTED decision never calls it
Status      : RESOLVED-IN-DIALOGUE
Stage       : P0        Module: INT        Version: v1
Lane        : analysis-dialogue · operator (single-operator converging dialogue)
Decided     : 2026-10-01T15:00:00+00:00
Dialogue-key: af2b07574c5c
traces      : POL-INT-007, POL-INT-008, POL-INT-009, POL-INT-010, POL-INT-011

## Decision
Raw idea §11 option 2: where the host exposes an Approval API, the service calls it after the employee confirms and records the report the approval was based on; §12 and G2: approval only as a result of the employee's action, only where the service enables it. REG supplies the approval API of a version to INT's decision path only (CON-REG-012); RPT records the decision with approvalApiExecuted and refuses the flag on a REJECTED decision (CON-RPT-006, RULE-RPT-014), and recommends 'call first, then record; a failed call records nothing' (ADR-RPT-003). Options: A) record first, call after — a recorded APPROVED decision the host never executed; B) call first, record after with approvalApiExecuted = true. Recommended B, guarded so the Approval API is never called for a Check RPT would refuse: INT reads the Check first (CON-RPT-003) and calls the Approval API only when the Check is COMPLETED and carries no decision. A failure (non-2xx) answers 502 and a timeout 504, recording nothing; the employee may decide again. If RPT still refuses after a successful call (a concurrent decision recorded in between), the refusal is answered with RPT's code and the call is logged with the Check identifier so the operator can reconcile with the host. Status: recommended — confirmed at prd-approval. Sources: [KB:raw-idea.md §11, §12]; domain-profile G2; CON-REG-012, CON-RPT-003, CON-RPT-006; ADR-RPT-003; profile 502/504.

Source      : governance-shared/analysis/modules/INT/_state/briefs/pass-1.md (P0 operator run)

<<<END ADR>>>

<<<ADR: ADR-INT-005.md>>>
# ADR-INT-005 — A manual upload carries one file of one document type, is accepted only while the Check is AWAITING_DOCUMENTS, and is handed to Document Access with the Check's service code and version read from the Report Store; the employee confirms the uploads as a separate action
Status      : RESOLVED-IN-DIALOGUE
Stage       : P0        Module: INT        Version: v1
Lane        : analysis-dialogue · operator (single-operator converging dialogue)
Decided     : 2026-10-01T15:00:00+00:00
Dialogue-key: d0fd9053299c
traces      : POL-INT-004, POL-INT-005, POL-INT-006

## Decision
Raw idea §6 `manual`: the employee uploads the files and the report is marked accordingly; §8 `POST /checks/{id}/documents`. DOC requires the service code and version number of the Check with every handover and refuses a non-manual service, a type not of the service and an incomplete upload (CON-DOC-003, ADR-DOC-006); CHK continues a manual Check only when INT confirms the uploads (CON-CHK-005). DOC does not know the Check's status. Options for status: A) accept uploads at any time — a file uploaded to a running or ended Check is never read and is deleted at the end (CON-DOC-005), silently; B) accept only while the Check is AWAITING_DOCUMENTS, read from RPT (CON-RPT-003), and refuse otherwise with an INT code. Recommended B. One file per upload keeps each refusal attached to one file; the employee uploads each document then confirms (screen_composition: upload and confirm are separate submits). Status: recommended — confirmed at prd-approval. Sources: [KB:raw-idea.md §6, §8, §12]; CON-DOC-003, CON-CHK-005, CON-RPT-003; profile `conventions.screen_composition`.

Source      : governance-shared/analysis/modules/INT/_state/briefs/pass-1.md (P0 operator run)

<<<END ADR>>>

<<<ADR: ADR-INT-006.md>>>
# ADR-INT-006 — The employee frontend is INT's host-facing frontend, opened from the host screen for one request with the service code, request number and employee identity the host passes; its four jobs — the Checks of a request, the report, the manual upload and the decision — are separate screens using only the REST API every host uses
Status      : RESOLVED-IN-DIALOGUE
Stage       : P0        Module: INT        Version: v1
Lane        : analysis-dialogue · operator (single-operator converging dialogue)
Decided     : 2026-10-01T15:00:00+00:00
Dialogue-key: 0dd46977757d
traces      : POL-INT-012, POL-INT-013, POL-INT-014, POL-INT-015, POL-INT-016, POL-INT-018

## Decision
A1 gives the employee, in a React + TypeScript frontend embedded in the host screen: the checks of a request, the report (overall status, findings with evidence, documents read / missing / unreadable), manual document upload and recording the decision; it consumes the same REST API as any host. Domain-profile §4 row 5 puts 'employee frontend's API surface' in INT and §7.2 makes the integration context 'everything a host or the employee frontend touches'. RPT has no screen (RPT module registry AUTO-DECISION). Options: A) one screen doing everything — mixes the upload and the decision in one submit, against `screen_composition`; B) four jobs, one screen each, with the host passing the request context at launch; a running Check's status is followed by polling RPT's read (raw idea §5). Recommended B. The frontend shows the report as RPT stores it: the Overall Status is CHK's derivation (a missing or unreadable required document already prevents COMPLIANT — CON-CHK-001), and the frontend never presents COMPLIANT beside a missing or unreadable required document (AIAS-11). Screen design belongs to P3.2. Status: recommended — confirmed at prd-approval. Sources: [KB:raw-idea.md §5, §15 A1]; domain-profile §4, §7.2; profile `stack.frontend`, `screen_composition`; review AIAS-11.

Source      : governance-shared/analysis/modules/INT/_state/briefs/pass-1.md (P0 operator run)

<<<END ADR>>>

<<<ADR: ADR-INT-007.md>>>
# ADR-INT-007 — Host Integration keeps no records of its own — no entity and no table; what INT does is recorded by the module that owns the fact, and the outcome of an Approval API call is recorded only as the decision's approvalApiExecuted flag in the Report Store
Status      : RESOLVED-IN-DIALOGUE
Stage       : P0        Module: INT        Version: v1
Lane        : analysis-dialogue · operator (single-operator converging dialogue)
Decided     : 2026-10-01T15:00:00+00:00
Dialogue-key: 656c3bc3a03c
traces      : POL-INT-017

## Decision
INT's four operations each end in another module's record: the Check run (RPT, through CHK), the Uploaded Document (DOC), the Employee Decision (RPT). Options: A) an INT log table of requests or approval calls — a second copy of facts RPT already holds, a retention rule of its own, and data carried beyond one request (G9); B) no INT storage; a failed Approval API call is answered to the employee and written to the application log with the Check identifier, and a successful one is recorded by RPT with the decision. Recommended B. If a later version needs an audit of approval calls, it adds an entity then. Status: recommended — confirmed at prd-approval. Sources: [KB:raw-idea.md §9, §11, §12]; domain-profile G9; CON-RPT-006; ADR-RPT-003.

Source      : governance-shared/analysis/modules/INT/_state/briefs/pass-1.md (P0 operator run)

<<<END ADR>>>

<<<ADR: ADR-INT-008.md>>>
# ADR-INT-008 — INT reads the approval API definition of a Check's service package version from REG in-process (CON-REG-012); the platform block keeps the owner's INT depends_on [CHK, RPT, DOC], and the INT → REG read is declared at entity level in the SRS
Status      : RESOLVED-IN-DIALOGUE
Stage       : P0        Module: INT        Version: v1
Lane        : analysis-dialogue · operator (single-operator converging dialogue)
Decided     : 2026-10-01T15:00:00+00:00
Dialogue-key: bb567d40c34b
traces      : POL-INT-009

## Decision
REG's contract reserves the approval API definition for INT's Employee Decision operation (CON-REG-012; ADR-REG-008) and leaves it out of the package CHK reads (CON-REG-007), so INT must read REG to know whether a version enables the Approval API. The owner's platform graph, kept identical in every module's platform summary, lists INT depends_on [CHK, RPT, DOC]. Options: A) change INT's platform row — diverges from the four existing platform summaries; B) keep the owner's platform row and declare the finer INT → REG (ENT-REG-002) SOFT-READ in the SRS `module-dependencies` block, which `gov.py graph` folds in. The edge targets tier 0 from tier 4, so the tier rule holds and the build order is unchanged (REG is already upstream of CHK, DOC and RPT). Recommended B. Status: recommended — confirmed at prd-approval. Sources: CON-REG-012, CON-REG-007; ADR-REG-008; RPT platform summary (owner graph); factory `xm.graph`.

Source      : governance-shared/analysis/modules/INT/_state/briefs/pass-1.md (P0 operator run)

<<<END ADR>>>

<<<ADR: ADR-INT-009.md>>>
# ADR-INT-009 — The Approval API is called with the method and path of the version's approval API definition, the request number filled into the path as one encoded value, against the host base address of the environment's configuration, within a configured timeout, once — never retried automatically; any 2xx answer is success
Status      : RESOLVED-IN-DIALOGUE
Stage       : P0        Module: INT        Version: v1
Lane        : analysis-dialogue · operator (single-operator converging dialogue)
Decided     : 2026-10-01T15:00:00+00:00
Dialogue-key: 204b82afd504
traces      : POL-INT-009, POL-INT-010

## Decision
Raw idea §4 shows `approval: {enabled, api: POST /requests/{requestId}/approve}`; REG supplies `approvalEnabled` and the `approvalApi` text (CON-REG-012). The host's base address differs per environment, like connections (§4 'set at activation time for each environment'). An approval is not idempotent on the host side, so an automatic retry could approve twice. Options: A) retry on failure — risk of double execution; B) one call; failure → 502, timeout → 504, the employee retries deliberately. Recommended B. The request number is substituted as a single URL-encoded path value, never concatenated as free text (G4 spirit); the call body carries the Check identifier and the deciding employee so the host can link the approval to the report it was based on (§11). The timeout is platform configuration (recommended default 10 seconds). No credential handling is specified in this version (A2). Status: recommended — confirmed at prd-approval. Sources: [KB:raw-idea.md §4, §11, §12, §15 A2]; CON-REG-012; profile 502/504.

Source      : governance-shared/analysis/modules/INT/_state/briefs/pass-1.md (P0 operator run)

<<<END ADR>>>

<<<ADR: ADR-INT-010.md>>>
# ADR-INT-010 — The checks INT runs before an Approval API call refuse with the Report Store's own codes and messages; without an Approval API call INT hands the decision straight to the Report Store and passes its refusal through
Status      : ACCEPTED
Stage       : P1        Module: INT        Version: v1
Context     : The Report Store refuses an incomplete decision, a decision on a Check that is not COMPLETED and a second decision (CON-RPT-006: RULE-RPT-011, RULE-RPT-012, RULE-RPT-013). ADR-INT-004 requires the Approval API never to be called for a decision the Report Store would refuse, so INT must evaluate those same conditions itself before the call — from the decision request and the Check read (CON-RPT-003). ADR-INT-003 gives INT codes only for what INT itself decides; these conditions are the Report Store's rules, evaluated early.
Decision    : (1) Where an APPROVED decision would be executed through the Approval API, INT refuses before the call: a decision code outside EMPLOYEE_DECISION or a missing deciding employee → `RPT-400-DECISION-INCOMPLETE`; a Check that is not COMPLETED → `RPT-409-CHECK-NOT-COMPLETED`; a Check that already holds a decision → `RPT-409-DECISION-ALREADY-RECORDED`; an unknown Check → `RPT-404-CHECK-NOT-FOUND` — each with the Report Store's message text (RULE-INT-002, RULE-INT-003). (2) Where no Approval API call is made (REJECTED, or a version that does not enable it), INT does not repeat these checks: it hands the decision to the Report Store, which refuses with the same codes. The caller therefore sees one code per situation whichever step detected it. (3) The upload refusal for a Check not waiting for documents stays INT's own code `INT-409-CHECK-NOT-AWAITING-DOCUMENTS` (ADR-INT-003, ADR-INT-005), because Document Access has no such rule.
Consequences: No INT code duplicates a Report Store refusal. P3.1 lists the four RPT codes in INT's error catalog as pass-through rows owned by RPT, raised early by INT on the approval path. If RPT changes a message, INT follows the RPT contract.
traces      : REQ-INT-006, REQ-INT-034, REQ-INT-035, RULE-INT-002, RULE-INT-003, US-INT-002, US-INT-006

<<<END ADR>>>

<<<ADR: ADR-INT-011.md>>>
# ADR-INT-011 — The employee frontend is opened by the host with the service code, request number and employee identity of one request; it shows the Overall Status as stored, never presents COMPLIANT beside a MISSING document outcome, shows every text as plain text, refreshes a Check that has not ended every 5 seconds, and reads its lists from their owners' HTTP reads
Status      : ACCEPTED
Stage       : P1        Module: INT        Version: v1
Context     : A1 embeds the frontend in the host screen for one request; the employee identity is passed by the host and recorded as sent (A2). The Overall Status is derived by CHK — a missing or unreadable required document gives a finding that is not SATISFIED, so the report is not COMPLIANT (CON-CHK-001, CON-CHK-002) — and the Report Store refuses to store COMPLIANT unless every finding is SATISFIED (RULE-RPT-005). AIAS-11 requires that a report with a missing or unreadable required document is never displayed as COMPLIANT. Document Access gives a MISSING outcome only for a required document type that no document carries (CON-DOC-004). The reads the screens need are served by RPT (Check, Checks of a request), REG (the service with its required document types) and DOC (uploaded documents of a Check).
Decision    : (1) Launch context: the host opens the frontend with a service code, a request number and an employee identity; without all three the frontend shows no Check and the RULE-INT-004 message. The employee identity of the launch is the one sent when the employee starts a Check or records a decision. (2) The frontend never derives an Overall Status; it presents the stored one. As a display safeguard, a report holding a MISSING document outcome is never presented as COMPLIANT — it is presented as "Not verified — a required document is missing" — and every UNREADABLE document is shown with its reason beside the status. (3) Evidence, notes, conditions, details and file names are shown as plain text, never interpreted as markup or followed as links (raw idea §12 'document content is data'). (4) While a shown Check is AWAITING_DOCUMENTS or RUNNING, its read is repeated every 5 seconds (frontend configuration) and stops when it is COMPLETED or FAILED. (5) The document type choices of an upload are the required document types of the Check's service from REG's service read; a type the Check's own version does not hold is refused by Document Access (DOC-422-DOCUMENT-TYPE-NOT-OF-SERVICE).
Consequences: P3.2 designs five screens (SCR-REQ-INT-001 … SCR-REQ-INT-005) on INT's four write operations and the owners' reads; no INT read operation is added. The polling interval is a frontend configuration value.
traces      : REQ-INT-016, REQ-INT-023, REQ-INT-040, REQ-INT-041, REQ-INT-049, REQ-INT-051, REQ-INT-055, RULE-INT-004, US-INT-008, US-INT-009, US-INT-010

<<<END ADR>>>

<<<ADR: ADR-INT-012.md>>>
# ADR-INT-012 — Host Integration's limits are platform configuration: the Approval API timeout (default 10 seconds), the upload request limit (default 50 MB, never set below Document Access's maximum file size) and the host Approval API base address per environment
Status      : ACCEPTED
Stage       : P1        Module: INT        Version: v1
Context     : Raw idea §12 gives every Check limits; the platform already keeps limits as configuration (ADR-REG-006). INT adds two outbound/inbound limits of its own: how long to wait for the host Approval API (profile 504) and how large an upload request may be before it is read. Document Access accepts a file above its maximum file size as a record without content and reports it UNREADABLE / TOO_LARGE (CON-DOC-003), so the transport limit must not cut such files off below that size. The host's base address differs per environment, as connections do (raw idea §4).
Decision    : (1) `approval timeout` — whole seconds, default 10; reaching it answers `INT-504-APPROVAL-API-TIMED-OUT`. (2) `upload request limit` — default 50 MB, configured at or above Document Access's maximum file size; a larger request answers `INT-413-UPLOAD-TOO-LARGE` and nothing reaches Document Access. (3) `approval base address` — per environment; the version's approval API definition supplies only the method and the path. None of these is an entity.
Consequences: P3.1 declares the three values in the CORE configuration; tests set them explicitly.
traces      : REQ-INT-014, REQ-INT-036, REQ-INT-037, US-INT-003, US-INT-007

<<<END ADR>>>

<<<ADR: ADR-INT-013.md>>>
# ADR-INT-013 — Host Integration declares no entity of its own; its rules name as data source the fields of the Report Store's Check Run and the Service Registry's Service Package Version that it reads through their contracts, and it owns no lookup
Status      : ACCEPTED
Stage       : P1        Module: INT        Version: v1
Context     : ADR-INT-007 gives INT no storage. The SRS template asks every RULE for a `Data source` naming declared entity fields, and the A6 lookups block for every lookup the module owns. INT's rules read the Check's status, decision and launch identifiers (CON-RPT-003 → ENT-RPT-001) and the version's approval flag (CON-REG-012 → ENT-REG-002).
Decision    : (1) A3 declares no ENT-INT; it lists the consumed fields INT reads, by their owners' ids. (2) Every RULE's `Data source` names those consumed fields (ENT-RPT-001.checkStatus, ENT-RPT-001.employeeDecision, ENT-RPT-001.decidedBy, ENT-RPT-001.serviceCode, ENT-RPT-001.requestNumber, ENT-RPT-001.employeeId). (3) `module-dependencies` consumes ENT-RPT-001 (RPT) and ENT-REG-002 (REG), both SOFT-READ through the in-process interfaces; CHK and DOC promise no entity INT reads, so the INT → CHK and INT → DOC edges stay the platform edges. (4) `lookups: []` — every code INT passes is owned by CHK, DOC, RPT or REG.
Consequences: P2 creates no table; its db-script records the data-source bindings to the owners' fields and the cross-module edges as XM records, with no column and no constraint.
traces      : RULE-INT-001, RULE-INT-002, RULE-INT-003, RULE-INT-004, US-INT-003, US-INT-006, US-INT-008, US-INT-012

<<<END ADR>>>

<<<ADR: ADR-INT-014.md>>>
# ADR-INT-014 — INT's contract promises its four host-facing operations — to host systems and the employee frontend — and no entity, because no module of the platform consumes INT
Status      : ACCEPTED
Stage       : P1.5      Module: INT        Version: v1
Context     : The contract engine promises what other modules may depend on: entities another module's A8 block consumes and operations another module calls. INT is tier 4, the top of the platform graph; no module's `module-dependencies` block consumes INT, and INT declares no entity (ADR-INT-013). INT's real consumers are outside the module graph: the host systems that call the REST API and the employee frontend that uses the same API (raw idea §8, §15 A1; ADR-INT-001). Their frontend and test stages build against these four operations, and the profile wants every backend block of a promised item to state `Honours: CON-…`.
Decision    : The contract carries four operation items — start a Check, hand over an upload, confirm the uploads, record an Employee Decision — each as a signature with its errors by meaning and their codes (ADR-INT-003, ADR-INT-010), no path and no verb. It carries no entity item and no lookup item: every code it names belongs to CHK, DOC, RPT or REG and is promised in their contracts. `Read by` names the host systems and INT's own frontend track.
Consequences: P3.1 implements each item and states `Honours: CON-INT-001 … CON-INT-004`; P3.2 binds the employee frontend's write calls to them. A later module that calls INT in-process would read the same items.
traces      : REQ-INT-001, REQ-INT-009, REQ-INT-018, REQ-INT-021, REQ-INT-057

<<<END ADR>>>

<<<ADR: ADR-INT-015.md>>>
# ADR-INT-015 — INT's db-script creates no table; its DBF matrix binds, read-only, the owners' columns INT reads through their contracts, so the factory's required DBF matrix and the RULE data sources resolve without inventing INT storage
Status      : ACCEPTED
Stage       : P2        Module: INT        Version: v1
Context     : ADR-INT-007 and ADR-INT-013 give INT no entity and no table (POL-INT-017, REQ-INT-059 — approved). The factory nevertheless requires every db-script to carry a `dbf-matrix` block with at least one row (factory `outputs.blocks`, C0.1), binds every RULE `Data source` field to the db-script (C6.9), and asks every backend API block to trace to a DBF (C7.3). Creating an INT table to satisfy the form would contradict the approved PRD (US-INT-012) — a breaking change. INT does read ten fields of other modules: the Check Run's identifier, status, service code, version number, request number, employee identity, decision and deciding employee (RPT, through CON-RPT-003 / CON-RPT-006) and the version's approval flag and approval API (REG, through CON-REG-012).
Decision    : (1) The FULL_DATABASE_SCRIPT holds no DDL and no DML — only comments stating that INT creates nothing; it runs as a no-op. (2) The DBF matrix carries one row per field INT reads, DBF-INT-001 … DBF-INT-010, each naming the owner's physical table and column as the owner's db-script defines them (RPT_CHECK_RUN, REG_SVC_PKG_VER) and the owner's ENT field; every row is a READ BINDING — the column is created, written and owned by RPT or REG, and INT neither creates, alters nor writes it, and never reads the table directly: the value arrives through the owner's in-process operation (XM-INT-001, XM-INT-002). `nullable` and `default` are left out because INT sets neither. (3) The two cross-module edges are SOFT-READ XM records, CONTRACTED on CON-RPT-001 and CON-REG-002. (4) The minimal-storage alternative (an INT approval-call log) is rejected: it breaks POL-INT-017; the executed-approval case of REQ-INT-039 goes to the service log.
Consequences: The backend plan binds by DBF-INT ids only and states, for C7.19, that INT writes none of them (owner-written). If a later version needs an INT audit record, it adds an entity and the first real DBF rows then.
traces      : REQ-INT-011, REQ-INT-034, REQ-INT-035, REQ-INT-041, REQ-INT-059, RULE-INT-001, RULE-INT-002, RULE-INT-003, RULE-INT-004

<<<END ADR>>>

<<<ADR: ADR-INT-016.md>>>
# ADR-INT-016 — INT reaches the Report Store through its operations and holds the Check identifier by value, so the INT → RPT dependency is the platform edge and not an entity-level XM; only the INT → REG read stays an entity-level edge (supersedes the RPT part of ADR-INT-013 (3) and ADR-INT-015 (3))
Status      : ACCEPTED
Stage       : P1        Module: INT        Version: v1
Context     : The first P1/P2 run declared two entity-level edges — RPT · ENT-RPT-001 and REG · ENT-REG-002 — as SOFT-READ XM records. Preparing P3.1 showed that this makes the Report Store an integration target, so outside the CROSS-MOD phase the plan may not name ENT-RPT-001 (C7.27), while every API block must name the entity its screen works on — the Check Run, ENT-RPT-001 (C7.23); the two clauses cannot both hold. The platform's own precedent settles which declaration is right: the Check identifier is the one RPT item 'every module holds by value' (CON-RPT-001), and CHK and DOC hold it with no XM; a module that calls another module's operations, as CHK calls Document Access, carries that dependency as the platform edge (CHK SRS A8; ADR-DOC-003). INT calls the Report Store's read and decision operations (CON-RPT-003, CON-RPT-006) and stores nothing of it. The INT → REG read is different: REG is not in INT's platform row, so the entity-level edge is the only place it is recorded (ADR-INT-008).
Decision    : (1) SRS A8 `module-dependencies` consumes only `{module: REG, entity: ENT-REG-002, type: SOFT-READ}`; the INT → RPT dependency is the platform edge INT depends_on RPT, like INT → CHK and INT → DOC. (2) P2's XM register carries one record, XM-INT-001 — REG · ENT-REG-002, SOFT-READ, CONTRACTED on CON-REG-002. The former XM-INT-001 (RPT) is withdrawn and the REG edge, first numbered XM-INT-002, takes XM-INT-001: v1 has not been planned against or published downstream yet, and the XM sequence may carry no gap (C6.8). (3) The DBF read bindings of ADR-INT-015 are unchanged; the eight RPT-column bindings are reached through the Report Store's operations over the platform edge. (4) The backend plan names the Check Run (ENT-RPT-001) as the subject of its API blocks and reaches REG only through XM-INT-001.
Consequences: The graph keeps INT → RPT (platform), INT → CHK (platform), INT → DOC (platform) and INT → REG (entity-level, XM-INT-001). P3.1 places one XM block (REG). If a later version makes INT store a reference to a Check Run, an entity-level RPT edge returns then.
traces      : US-INT-003, US-INT-005, US-INT-006, REQ-INT-010, REQ-INT-021, REQ-INT-025, REQ-INT-030, ENT-REG-002

<<<END ADR>>>

<<<ADR: ADR-INT-017.md>>>
# ADR-INT-017 — INT's backend is four POST endpoints with no repository and no query catalog; refusals of CHK, DOC and RPT are pass-through catalog rows; the only network call is the host Approval API port; approval-path decisions on one Check are serialised in-process; Arabic messages pending
Status      : ACCEPTED
Stage       : P3.1      Module: INT        Version: v1
Context     : INT declares no entity and creates no table (ADR-INT-007, ADR-INT-015); its four contract operations (CON-INT-001 … CON-INT-004) orchestrate the in-process operations of CHK, DOC, RPT and REG and one outbound HTTP call. The engine template expects repositories (QR), PLATFORM-STD rows with an ADR, screen operations resolved to endpoints (C7.23), and a concurrency answer for every read-then-write. The SRS screens also name operations served by other modules' HTTP reads (list, read), which INT must not duplicate (ADR-INT-001). The SRS gives English messages only (profile languages en, ar; require_all false), as CHK, DOC and RPT found (ADR-CHK-018, ADR-DOC-012, ADR-RPT-013).
Decision    : (1) Endpoints: POST /api/v1/checks (202), POST /api/v1/checks/{checkId}/documents (multipart, 201), POST /api/v1/checks/{checkId}/upload-confirmation (202), POST /api/v1/checks/{checkId}/decision (201) — API-INT-001 … API-INT-004, each honouring its CON-INT item. No GET is added. (2) No QR: INT has no repository; every read and write is another module's in-process operation, named in the PORTS phase (CHK, DOC, RPT — platform edges) or in the CROSS-MOD block (REG — XM-INT-001). (3) Error catalog: INT's own codes INT-400-REQUEST-INVALID, INT-409-CHECK-NOT-AWAITING-DOCUMENTS (RULE-INT-001), INT-413-UPLOAD-TOO-LARGE, INT-500, INT-502-APPROVAL-API-FAILED, INT-504-APPROVAL-API-TIMED-OUT (PLATFORM-STD rows cite this ADR); the in-process refusals of CHK, DOC and RPT are catalog rows with rule `PASS-THROUGH` (ADR-INT-003) or the INT rule that raises them early (RULE-INT-002, RULE-INT-003 — ADR-INT-010), with the owner's code, status and message. (4) Screen operations: `create` and `custom` are built by API-INT-001 … API-INT-004 on the Check Run (ENT-RPT-001); `list` and `read` are not built by INT — derived: served by the owners' HTTP reads the frontend calls directly. (5) The host Approval API is the only network call: a port with an HTTP adapter, base address and timeout from CORE configuration, one call, no retry (ADR-INT-009, ADR-INT-012). (6) Concurrency: two APPROVED decisions on one approval-enabled Check would both pass the pre-call checks and both call the host; INT serialises the approval path per Check identifier with an in-process lock (one deployable) and re-reads the Check inside it; the Report Store's conditional update stays the final guard. A multi-instance deployment needs the host Approval API to tolerate a repeated approval — stated, not solved, in v1. (7) Arabic messages are `PENDING ADR-INT-017`.
Consequences: P3.2 binds the frontend's writes to the four operations of api-spec-int.yaml and its reads to the RPT, REG and DOC documents. P4 derives INT test cases from the ACs through these four operations and a stub host Approval API.
traces      : REQ-INT-001, REQ-INT-009, REQ-INT-014, REQ-INT-018, REQ-INT-021, REQ-INT-033, REQ-INT-035, REQ-INT-036, REQ-INT-037, REQ-INT-039

<<<END ADR>>>

<<<ADR: ADR-INT-018.md>>>
# ADR-INT-018 — The employee frontend binds its writes to INT's four operations and its reads to the owners' documents by id (API-RPT-001, API-RPT-002, API-REG-002, API-DOC-001); the launch context travels in the URL; the confirmation screen also reads the required document types; no operation reads the required document types of a Check's own version (recorded gap); the MISSING safeguard stays as ADR-INT-011 decided
Status      : ACCEPTED
Stage       : P3.2      Module: INT        Version: v1
Context     : INT owns the five employee screens (raw-idea A1; ADR-INT-006, ADR-INT-011). Its API document `api-spec-int.yaml` holds only the four write operations (API-INT-001 … API-INT-004 — ADR-INT-001, ADR-INT-017); every read the screens need is published by its owner: the Report Store (`api-spec-rpt.yaml` — API-RPT-001 read a Check and its report, API-RPT-002 the Checks of a request, API-RPT-003 the decision agreement), the Service Registry (`api-spec-reg.yaml` — API-REG-001 list services, API-REG-002 read one service, API-REG-003 the load report), Document Access (`api-spec-doc.yaml` — API-DOC-001 the uploaded documents of a Check) and the Check Engine (`api-spec-chk.yaml` — API-CHK-001 the Active Check). RPT, REG and DOC state their consumer surfaces in their own P3.2 plans and mint no screen (ADR-RPT-014, ADR-REG-012, ADR-DOC-013). Four points are not settled by the SRS: (a) which read operation serves each screen read and which published reads stay unused; (b) API-REG-002 answers the service's CURRENT version only, while REQ-INT-016 and REQ-INT-020 speak of the required document types of the Check's service, and the Check may run an earlier version (AC-INT-014); (c) the SRS B5 table of SCR-REQ-INT-004 lists only the uploaded-documents read, although REQ-INT-020 shows "the required document types that have no upload"; (d) how the launch context (service code, request number, employee identity — ADR-INT-011 (1)) survives navigation, back and reload inside the host screen. AIAS-11 and ADR-INT-006 require that a report with a missing or unreadable required document is never displayed as COMPLIANT; ADR-INT-011 (2) and REQ-INT-049 resolved that as "stored status, MISSING safeguard, every UNREADABLE document shown with its reason beside the status".
Decision    : (1) Reads by id: SCR-INT-001 reads API-RPT-002; SCR-INT-002 reads API-RPT-001 (and polls it every 5 seconds while the status is AWAITING_DOCUMENTS or RUNNING, stopping at COMPLETED or FAILED — ADR-INT-011 (4)); SCR-INT-003 reads API-RPT-001 (service code, status), API-REG-002 (required document types) and API-DOC-001 (uploaded documents); SCR-INT-004 reads API-RPT-001, API-DOC-001 and API-REG-002; SCR-INT-005 reads API-RPT-001 (status and decision, so the decision is offered only on a COMPLETED, undecided Check — REQ-INT-054). Writes: API-INT-001 (SCR-INT-001), API-INT-002 (SCR-INT-003), API-INT-003 (SCR-INT-004), API-INT-004 (SCR-INT-005). The mock server of the frontend executor serves `api-spec-int.yaml` (the `api-surface` block's `mock`) together with `api-spec-rpt.yaml`, `api-spec-reg.yaml` and `api-spec-doc.yaml` for the reads. Unused, mapped to no INT REQ: API-RPT-003 (administrator read — ADR-RPT-014 (4)), API-REG-001, API-REG-003 (no screen lists services or load results — no administration UI, raw-idea §2) and API-CHK-001 (it answers 404 once a Check has ended, so it cannot drive REQ-INT-056; API-RPT-001 carries the status of every Check). (2) Gap recorded, not invented: no published operation reads the required document types of a given service package version. Until one exists, the choices and the "no upload" list come from API-REG-002 (current version), as ADR-INT-011 (5) decided; a type the Check's own version does not require is refused by Document Access with DOC-422-DOCUMENT-TYPE-NOT-OF-SERVICE, whose detail names the version's own required types and is shown as received. The missing operation belongs to REG's API document (`GET` of one version's summary) and is named here for a later REG version. (3) SCR-INT-004 reads API-REG-002 in addition to the SRS B5 row (reconciliation B3: a read REQ-INT-020 needs is added, not a field). (4) The host opens the frontend at its base route with the query parameters `serviceCode`, `requestNumber` and `employeeId`; every route keeps them in its query string, so back, reload and a deep link keep the launch context; RULE-INT-004 is checked on every route before any read. (5) The display safeguard is ADR-INT-011 (2) unchanged: a report holding a MISSING document outcome is presented as "Not verified — a required document is missing", never COMPLIANT; an UNREADABLE outcome is shown with its reason and detail on the documents pane beside the stored Overall Status (the Report Store never stores COMPLIANT unless every finding is SATISFIED — RULE-RPT-005 — and an unreadable required document yields a finding that is not SATISFIED, so the stored status already prevents COMPLIANT); no new copy is invented for UNREADABLE. (6) Field labels are given in en and ar in the ui-ux-spec; message texts are the SRS's (en) and the catalogue's, ar `PENDING ADR-INT-017`. (7) The factory clauses C9.5 (traces → API defined in `api-spec`) and C9.24 (ux-reads-spec) resolve API ids against this module's own document only; the four foreign read ids above resolve in their owners' documents and are reported by those clauses until the factory resolves cross-module documents. They are cited, not hidden, because REQ-INT-057 requires every operation the frontend calls to be a published one.
Consequences: P3.2 plans the screens on 4 INT operations and 4 owner reads; the frontend test plan's integration cases (INT-UXD) exercise the owner reads through UXD-INT-001 … UXD-INT-008. A later REG version adding a per-version read closes gap (2) without changing a screen. Non-breaking: no REQ, RULE or locked decision is contradicted.
traces      : REQ-INT-016, REQ-INT-017, REQ-INT-020, REQ-INT-040, REQ-INT-041, REQ-INT-045, REQ-INT-049, REQ-INT-054, REQ-INT-055, REQ-INT-056, REQ-INT-057, RULE-INT-004

<<<END ADR>>>

<<<ADR: ADR-INT-019.md>>>
# ADR-INT-019 — INT's test plans: each AC is derived on the track that can observe it (43 backend, 23 frontend); three backend ACs get a frontend case as well so every screen package of SCR-INT-005 has acceptance; MODEL-EVAL is not populated by INT; the host Approval API is a stub
Status      : ACCEPTED
Stage       : P4        Module: INT        Version: v1
Context     : The P4 engine derives one TC per AC, one or two per XM edge and per UXD, and requires every split unit of each execution plan to be named by ≥1 TC of its track (C10.11). INT's ACs split between behaviour only an API call can observe (starts, uploads, confirmations, decisions, refusals, the Approval API call — 43 ACs) and behaviour only the screens can observe (the Checks of a request, the report, the upload and confirmation screens, the deciding identity, polling, plain text, the network traffic of the frontend — 23 ACs). The frontend plan has 20 per-screen units (F1–F4 × SCR-INT-001 … SCR-INT-005); the Employee decision screen (SCR-INT-005) is the subject of a single frontend AC (AC-INT-027), while its F1, F2 and F4 units implement the recorded-decision answer (REQ-INT-022), the refusal routing of an Approval API failure (REQ-INT-036) and the retry (REQ-INT-038) that SCR-REQ-INT-005 traces. The profile's TEST-PLAN-BE phase names a MODEL-EVAL group (raw-idea §10, AIAS-10): the fixed known-result request set run on every model change. INT calls no model (backend plan PORTS: "INT has no query port, no document reader and no model port"). The host Approval API is outside the service.
Decision    : (1) Each AC yields its TC on the track that observes it: backend TC-INT-001 … TC-INT-043 (43 ACs) and TC-INT-044 for XM-INT-001; frontend TC-INT-045 … TC-INT-086 (23 ACs, 3 frontend twins, 16 UXD cases). (2) AC-INT-026, AC-INT-041 and AC-INT-043 also yield one frontend case each (TC-INT-069, TC-INT-065, TC-INT-070) on SCR-INT-005, asserting what the screen shows for the same Then — no new behaviour, no invented data. (3) A frontend case's package is the SUB of the screen part that proves it: models (F1) for a foreign field rendering the served value, data hooks (F2) for a read failure, polling or a mutation's refusal routing, forms and validators (F3) for the launch-context check, the status presenter, plain text and the form bindings, screens and routes (F4) for what a screen shows and offers. (4) MODEL-EVAL is absent from INT's backend test plan: INT has no model; the MODEL-EVAL set belongs to the module that runs the comparison (CHK). (5) Backend cases run against a stub host Approval API (configurable answer status and delay, recording every call) at `aias.integration.approval.base-address`; the owner modules run in-process as delivered. Frontend cases run against the mock server that serves api-spec-int.yaml and the owners' documents (ADR-INT-018 (1)). (6) Arabic message texts are asserted as `PENDING ADR-INT-017` until the SRS carries them.
Consequences: AC coverage 66/66, XM coverage 1/1, UXD coverage 8/8; every backend unit with tests (PORTS, SVC-API, XM-INT-001) and every frontend SUB is named by ≥1 TC. Non-breaking: no REQ, RULE or locked decision is contradicted.
traces      : REQ-INT-022, REQ-INT-023, REQ-INT-036, REQ-INT-038, REQ-INT-057, AC-INT-026, AC-INT-027, AC-INT-041, AC-INT-043, XM-INT-001

<<<END ADR>>>

<<<ADR: ADR-INT-020.md>>>
# ADR-INT-020 — INT is the single REST surface of the employee frontend: four read operations are added (a Check and its report, the Checks of a request, the uploaded documents of a Check without content, the required document types of the Check's own version), each served in-process through the owner's published contract; supersedes the "no read operation" part of ADR-INT-001 and ADR-INT-017 (1); the owners' own reads stay; the uploaded-documents read waits on a DOC contract operation (recorded gap)
Status      : ACCEPTED
Stage       : P1        Module: INT        Version: v1
Context     : ADR-INT-001 kept every read with its owner, and ADR-INT-017 (1) said "No GET is added", so the employee frontend's screens read four foreign HTTP operations (API-RPT-001, API-RPT-002, API-REG-002, API-DOC-001 — ADR-INT-018). The factory builds and mocks each module's frontend against that module's OWN API document (C9.24 requires the plan's mock block to name `api-spec-int.yaml`; C9.5 resolves every API id the plan cites in that document), so a screen bound to a foreign read cannot be built or mocked as specified: 11 CRITICAL findings at P3.2. Raw-idea §14 places "the REST API" in INT, and the domain profile puts "the employee frontend's API surface" in INT (§4 row 5, §7.2 — ADR-INT-006). ADR-INT-018 (2) also recorded a gap: REG's HTTP read answers the CURRENT version only, while REQ-INT-016 and REQ-INT-020 need the required document types of the Check's own service, which may run an earlier version (AC-INT-014). The owners publish these in-process operations: the Report Store `readCheck(checkId)` (CON-RPT-003) and `listChecksOfRequest(serviceCode, requestNumber)` (CON-RPT-004); the Service Registry `getServicePackageVersion(serviceCode, versionNumber)` with `requiredDocumentTypes` (CON-REG-009, on ENT-REG-002 — the entity INT already consumes, A8). Document Access publishes no in-process operation that lists the uploaded documents of a Check: its contract has only the hand-over, the fetch and the end of a Check (CON-DOC-003 … CON-DOC-005), and its list is the HTTP read API-DOC-001 (ADR-DOC-011). The decision agreement (CON-RPT-005) is used by no INT screen.
Decision    : (1) INT adds four read requirements, REQ-INT-061 … REQ-INT-064 (AC-INT-067 … AC-INT-074), and P3.1 adds one GET operation each to `api-spec-int.yaml`: a Check and its report (through CON-RPT-003), the Checks of a request (through CON-RPT-004), the uploaded documents of a Check without content (through Document Access — see (4)), the required document types of the service package version the Check runs on (CON-RPT-003 for the Check's service code and version, then CON-REG-009). No read touches another module's table. (2) The frontend reads only INT's operations (REQ-INT-057 unchanged: INT's operations are REST API operations every host may call). The owners' own HTTP reads (API-RPT-001 … API-RPT-003, API-REG-001 … API-REG-003, API-DOC-001, API-CHK-001) stay as they are; INT's reads are the frontend-facing facade and add nothing to them. (3) Paths are distinct from the owners' (one service, one base path): the Report Store owns `GET /api/v1/checks` and `GET /api/v1/checks/{checkId}`, so INT's report reads sit under `/api/v1/check-reports`; the two Check-scoped reads sit under INT's own `/api/v1/checks/{checkId}/…` resources (final paths are P3.1's). (4) Gap recorded against DOC: Document Access must publish an in-process operation listing the uploaded documents of a Check without content (the fields of its API-DOC-001 answer: uploadedDocumentId, documentType, fileName, fileSize, oversized, upload time). Until DOC's contract carries it, INT's uploaded-documents read is planned and documented but its adapter is `PENDING ADR-INT-020`; INT never reads DOC's table. (5) The required document types of the Check's own version close ADR-INT-018 (2): the choice list of an upload and the "no upload" list of the confirmation are those of the version the Check runs on; this supersedes the current-version part of ADR-INT-011 (5). Document Access's refusal DOC-422-DOCUMENT-TYPE-NOT-OF-SERVICE stays the final guard. (6) Refusals of the owners' reads pass through unchanged in the standard error form (REQ-INT-006): RPT-404-CHECK-NOT-FOUND, RPT-400-REQUEST-KEYS-MISSING; a non-numeric Check identifier is INT-400-REQUEST-INVALID (REQ-INT-007). (7) No decision-agreement read is added: no INT screen shows it (ADR-RPT-014 (4)). (8) The SCR-REQ B5 rows name INT's read operations in place of the owners' reads; screen fields, rules and permissions are unchanged.
Consequences: P1.5 adds INT contract items for the four reads; P2 is unchanged (INT creates no table; the reads bind the owners' fields through their operations); P3.1 adds API-INT-005 … API-INT-008 and their catalog rows; P3.2 binds every screen read to INT's operations only (a new ADR supersedes ADR-INT-018 (1), (2), (7)); P4 derives TCs from AC-INT-067 … AC-INT-074. DOC's next version closes gap (4). Non-breaking: no REQ, RULE or confirmed decision is contradicted — ADR-INT-001's reasons (no duplicate storage, each fact with its owner) hold, since INT stores nothing and only relays the owners' operations.
traces      : REQ-INT-016, REQ-INT-017, REQ-INT-020, REQ-INT-040, REQ-INT-045, REQ-INT-057, REQ-INT-061, REQ-INT-062, REQ-INT-063, REQ-INT-064, US-INT-011

<<<END ADR>>>

<<<ADR: ADR-INT-021.md>>>
# ADR-INT-021 — Every screen read of the employee frontend binds INT's own read operations (API-INT-005 … API-INT-008); the mock server serves api-spec-int.yaml alone; the upload choices and the "no upload" list are the Check's own version's; supersedes ADR-INT-018 (1), (2), (3) and (7)
Status      : ACCEPTED
Stage       : P3.2      Module: INT        Version: v1
Context     : ADR-INT-018 bound the screens' reads to the owners' HTTP operations (API-RPT-001, API-RPT-002, API-REG-002, API-DOC-001), had the mock server serve four documents, recorded that no operation read the required document types of a Check's own version, and recorded that C9.5 / C9.24 report the foreign ids. ADR-INT-020 (P1 revision) made INT the single REST surface of the employee frontend: `api-spec-int.yaml` now carries API-INT-005 (a Check and its report), API-INT-006 (the Checks of a request), API-INT-007 (the uploaded documents of a Check, without content) and API-INT-008 (the required document types of the version the Check runs on), each relaying the owner's published operation.
Decision    : (1) Screen reads: SCR-INT-001 reads API-INT-006; SCR-INT-002 reads API-INT-005 (polled every 5 seconds while AWAITING_DOCUMENTS or RUNNING, stopped at COMPLETED or FAILED — ADR-INT-011 (4)); SCR-INT-003 reads API-INT-005 (status), API-INT-008 (choices) and API-INT-007 (uploaded documents); SCR-INT-004 reads API-INT-005, API-INT-007 and API-INT-008; SCR-INT-005 reads API-INT-005 (status and decision — REQ-INT-054). Writes unchanged: API-INT-001 … API-INT-004. No operation of another module's document is cited by the frontend plan. (2) The mock server of the frontend executor serves `api-spec-int.yaml` only. (3) The document type choices of an upload and the "no upload" list of the confirmation are the required document types of the version the Check runs on (API-INT-008); ADR-INT-018 (2)'s gap is closed; Document Access's DOC-422-DOCUMENT-TYPE-NOT-OF-SERVICE stays the final guard. (4) The screens' reads route INT's own codes: INT-400-REQUEST-INVALID, RPT-404-CHECK-NOT-FOUND and RPT-400-REQUEST-KEYS-MISSING (pass-through, owner's words), INT-500. (5) The UXD entries stay: the data's authoritative source is still the owner module, reached through INT's relay; each UXD names its owner module and the INT operation that serves it. (6) ADR-INT-018 (4), (5) and (6) — the launch context in the URL, the MISSING safeguard as ADR-INT-011 decided, en/ar labels with messages ar PENDING ADR-INT-017 — remain in force.
Consequences: The frontend plan binds only API-INT-* ids, so C9.5 and C9.24 resolve in `api-spec-int.yaml`; the frontend test plan runs against one mock document. API-INT-007's backend adapter waits on Document Access's in-process listing operation (ADR-INT-020 (4)); the frontend is built against the document and is unaffected. Non-breaking.
traces      : REQ-INT-016, REQ-INT-017, REQ-INT-020, REQ-INT-040, REQ-INT-045, REQ-INT-054, REQ-INT-055, REQ-INT-057, REQ-INT-061, REQ-INT-062, REQ-INT-063, REQ-INT-064

<<<END ADR>>>

<<<ADR: ADR-INT-022.md>>>
# ADR-INT-022 — INT's test plans after the read operations: AC-INT-067 … AC-INT-074 are backend cases TC-INT-087 … TC-INT-094 on the SVC-API-QUERY package; the write cases move to SVC-API-COMMAND; frontend cases run against api-spec-int.yaml alone; supersedes ADR-INT-019 (1) counts and (5) mock part
Status      : ACCEPTED
Stage       : P4        Module: INT        Version: v1
Context     : ADR-INT-020 added REQ-INT-061 … REQ-INT-064 (AC-INT-067 … AC-INT-074) and API-INT-005 … API-INT-008; with eight API blocks the backend plan's SVC-API phase reached its split threshold and now splits into SVC-API-COMMAND (the four writes) and SVC-API-QUERY (the four reads), so the unit `SVC-API` no longer exists. ADR-INT-021 binds every screen read to INT's operations, so the frontend mock serves `api-spec-int.yaml` only. ADR-INT-019 had derived 43 backend ACs and named a mock serving four documents. TC-INT-001 … TC-INT-086 were written in the same uncommitted P4 run.
Decision    : (1) The eight new ACs observe API responses, so each yields one backend case, TC-INT-087 … TC-INT-094 (continuing the sequence; no id is renumbered); the refusals (AC-INT-068, AC-INT-070, AC-INT-074) sit in RULE-SCENARIOS, the others in API-SCENARIOS; each names SVC-API-QUERY as its package. (2) Every backend case that named SVC-API names SVC-API-COMMAND; PORTS and XM-INT-001 cases are unchanged. (3) Frontend cases exercise INT's reads by id (API-INT-005 … API-INT-008) against the mock server serving `api-spec-int.yaml`; a failing read is INT's own INT-500 (the owners' read codes are no longer seen by the frontend). (4) Counts: backend 52 cases (51 ACs + XM-INT-001), frontend 42 cases (23 ACs, 3 frontend cases of backend ACs, 16 UXD cases); AC coverage 74/74. ADR-INT-019 (2), (3), (4) and (6) stay in force.
Consequences: Every unit with tests of both execution plans (PORTS, SVC-API-COMMAND, SVC-API-QUERY, XM-INT-001; F1–F4 × 5 screens) is named by ≥1 case. TC-INT-091 needs Document Access's in-process listing operation delivered (ADR-INT-020 (4)). Non-breaking.
traces      : REQ-INT-061, REQ-INT-062, REQ-INT-063, REQ-INT-064, AC-INT-067, AC-INT-068, AC-INT-069, AC-INT-070, AC-INT-071, AC-INT-072, AC-INT-073, AC-INT-074

<<<END ADR>>>

<<<ADR: ADR-INT-023.md>>>
# ADR-INT-023 — INT's uploaded-documents read (API-INT-007, CON-INT-007) binds Document Access's published in-process operation CON-DOC-006 `listUploadedDocuments(checkId)`; the adapter is no longer PENDING; the INT → DOC dependency stays the platform edge (no new XM); closes ADR-INT-020 (4)
Status      : ACCEPTED
Stage       : P1        Module: INT        Version: v1
Context     : ADR-INT-020 (4) recorded a gap against Document Access: no in-process operation listed the uploaded documents of a Check, so INT's read API-INT-007 (`GET /api/v1/checks/{checkId}/documents`, CON-INT-007, REQ-INT-063) was planned and documented with its adapter `PENDING ADR-INT-020`, and ADR-INT-021 / ADR-INT-022 noted that the backend adapter and TC-INT-091 waited on it. Document Access has now published CON-DOC-006 `listUploadedDocuments(checkId) → list of Uploaded Document Summary {uploadedDocumentId, documentType, fileName, fileSize, oversized, uploadedAt} ordered by uploadedAt` · errors: Check identifier absent (`DOC-400-CHECK-ID-REQUIRED`) (analysis/modules/DOC/P1_5/contract-doc.md#CON-DOC-006, ADR-DOC-017). An unknown or ended Check answers an empty list, not an error; the content is never returned; its notes name API-INT-007 as the consumer.
Decision    : (1) API-INT-007 / CON-INT-007 relay CON-DOC-006 unchanged: `DocumentAccessPort.listUploaded(checkId)` in `DocDocumentAccessAdapter` calls the injected `DocumentAccess.listUploadedDocuments(checkId)` and returns its list as received (same six fields, same upload order); INT never reads DOC's table (ENT-DOC-001 stays PRIVATE — ADR-DOC-003). (2) INT always passes a parsed, numeric Check identifier (a non-numeric one is INT-400-REQUEST-INVALID before any call — REQ-INT-007), so DOC-400-CHECK-ID-REQUIRED cannot arise from INT; should it ever be raised it is an unexpected failure answered INT-500 (REQ-INT-008) and adds no catalog row. (3) An unknown Check answers HTTP 200 with an empty list, as CON-DOC-006 promises (AC-INT-072 covers the empty case); INT adds no existence check of its own. (4) The dependency stays the platform edge INT depends_on DOC (ADR-INT-016): INT calls an operation and stores nothing of Document Access, and DOC's entities are PRIVATE, so no entity-level XM record is added; XM-INT-001 (REG) remains INT's only XM. (5) The "pending" wording of SRS A8 and REQ-INT-063's Source, CON-INT-007's notes, P2's list of reached operations, the PORTS adapter and the API-INT-007 block, and the open-ADR lines of the test plans are updated additively to cite CON-DOC-006; P4 adds one backend case TC-INT-095 for the binding (the adapter relays the operation's list unchanged and never returns content). (6) ADR-INT-020 (4) is closed by this ADR; ADR-INT-020 (1)–(3), (5)–(8), ADR-INT-021 and ADR-INT-022 stay in force.
Alternatives rejected: an entity-level XM SOFT-READ on ENT-DOC-001 — the entity is PRIVATE and INT holds no column of it (ADR-INT-016 reasoning); calling DOC's HTTP read API-DOC-001 — one deployable, in-process interface per profile.
Consequences: P1 (A8 and REQ-INT-063 source), P1.5 (CON-INT-007 notes), P2 (operations reached), P3.1 (PORTS, API-INT-007 block, registry gap line) and P4 (TC-INT-095; ADR lines) change additively; no REQ, AC, API or code is renumbered or removed. Non-breaking.
traces      : REQ-INT-017, REQ-INT-020, REQ-INT-063, AC-INT-071, AC-INT-072

<<<END ADR>>>

<<<ADR: ADR-INT-024.md>>>
# ADR-INT-024 — CHK v1 delivered: the Check Engine contract items INT cites (CON-CHK-001 … CON-CHK-005) are unchanged since INT's design, so INT's INT → CHK edge and every plan bound to it remain valid; resolves the XM event INT>CHK
Status      : ACCEPTED
Stage       : P1.5      Module: INT        Version: v1
Context     : The factory raised the XM resolution event INT>CHK (key INT>CHK@CHKv1:delivered) because the Check Engine (CHK) v1 was delivered and tagged after INT designed against it. INT depends on CHK over the platform edge INT depends_on CHK (ADR-INT-016) and cites these CHK contract items: CON-CHK-001 (Check status and Overall Status — closed lists), CON-CHK-002 (finding outcome), CON-CHK-003 (Check failure reason), CON-CHK-004 `startCheck(serviceCode, requestNumber, employeeId) → {checkId, status}` with its refusals (incomplete start, service not available, connection not activated) and CON-CHK-005 `confirmUploads(checkId) → {checkId, status}` with its refusals (Check not found, Check not waiting for documents). INT's PORTS adapter `ChkCheckEngineAdapter`, API-INT-001 and API-INT-003, the five CHK pass-through catalog rows (CHK-400-START-INCOMPLETE, CHK-422-SERVICE-NOT-AVAILABLE, CHK-422-CONNECTION-NOT-ACTIVATED, CHK-404-CHECK-NOT-FOUND, CHK-409-CHECK-NOT-AWAITING-DOCUMENTS) and their test cases are written against them. CON-CHK-006 … CON-CHK-011 (the Check result port and the Check reads) are RPT's and CHK's internal port and are not consumed by INT.
Decision    : (1) Verified: CHK's delivered contract (analysis/modules/CHK/P1_5/contract-chk.md, identical to CHK's _state/current-contract.md) last changed before INT's P0 began, and the five cited items carry the same signatures, closed code lists, refusals and messages INT's SRS A8, contract, backend plan (PORTS, API-INT-001, API-INT-003, error catalog) and test plans cite. No item INT depends on was added, changed or removed by the delivery. (2) The INT → CHK edge therefore remains valid as designed; no INT artifact changes and no plan is re-derived. (3) The event INT>CHK is answered with this ADR. A later CHK version that changes CON-CHK-001 … CON-CHK-005 raises a new event and is re-checked then.
Alternatives rejected: re-running INT's P1 … P4 against the delivered CHK — nothing INT cites changed, so a re-run would only reproduce the same artifacts.
Consequences: INT's XM event INT>CHK is resolved; INT's plans keep their CHK bindings unchanged. Non-breaking.
traces      : REQ-INT-001, REQ-INT-002, REQ-INT-006, REQ-INT-018

<<<END ADR>>>

<<<ADR: ADR-INT-025.md>>>
# ADR-INT-025 — Document Access v1's two further upload refusals, DOC-409-CHECK-ENDED and DOC-422-UPLOAD-LIMIT-REACHED, are passed through unchanged on the upload operation; closes platform finding PF-5 for INT
Status      : ACCEPTED
Stage       : P1        Module: INT        Version: v1
Context     : Document Access (DOC) v1, delivered and tagged doc-v1, refuses an upload handover (CON-DOC-003 `handOverUpload`) in six ways. INT's artifacts passed through only four of them (DOC-400-INCOMPLETE-UPLOAD, DOC-404-SERVICE-VERSION-NOT-FOUND, DOC-422-FETCH-MODE-NOT-MANUAL, DOC-422-DOCUMENT-TYPE-NOT-OF-SERVICE). DOC's revise added two more: "Check already ended" (RULE-DOC-009, ADR-DOC-015, in-process code DOC-409-CHECK-ENDED) and "maximum uploads per Check reached" (RULE-DOC-010, ADR-DOC-016, configuration `aias.check.max-uploads`, default 20, in-process code DOC-422-UPLOAD-LIMIT-REACHED). Neither appeared in INT's contract, API document, error catalog, adapter note or test cases, so either refusal would have fallen through to a misleading INT-500. Platform finding PF-5 names INT as the owner of listing DOC's in-process refusal codes in its own catalog and mapping them to ProblemDetail. The DOC-409 case is the race PF-6 describes: INT reads the Check as AWAITING_DOCUMENTS, the Check Engine then ends the Check and notifies DOC, and INT's handover arrives after the notice. (Analysis-gate round 1, finding G1.)
Decision    : (1) Both codes are PASS-THROUGH refusals under ADR-INT-003 and REQ-INT-006: the upload operation (API-INT-002, CON-INT-002) answers DOC's code, HTTP status (409 and 422 respectively) and RULE message unchanged as a ProblemDetail. INT neither mirrors DOC's upload limit nor checks whether DOC has recorded the Check as ended; DOC alone decides both. (2) SRS: AC-INT-075 (ended Check) and AC-INT-076 (upload limit) are added under REQ-INT-006. Contract: CON-INT-002's error list gains both codes. P3.1: API-INT-002's responses and x-error-codes list both, the error catalog gains two PASS-THROUGH rows (21 → 23, DOC pass-through 4 → 6), the DocumentAccessPort adapter note lists all six DOC upload refusals, and API-INT-002's concurrency note now says DOC refuses a handover for an ended Check synchronously (CheckEndedException → DOC-409-CHECK-ENDED); it does not accept the file and delete it later. P3.2: the upload screen shows both refusals as a form message with the server detail. P4: one backend case per code. (3) INT keeps its side of PF-6 (the ordering rule): it hands over an upload only after reading the Check as AWAITING_DOCUMENTS (REQ-INT-011, RULE-INT-001). The remaining race window ends in DOC's synchronous refusal, which INT passes through. (4) PF-5 is closed for INT by this ADR. The project-registry row is updated by the registry operator, not by this module's stages.
Alternatives rejected: mapping either refusal to an INT-owned code. ADR-INT-003 keeps the owner's code and status so the caller learns the reason in the owner's words. Mirroring DOC's upload limit in INT was also rejected: it would duplicate DOC's configuration and its count, and the two could drift.
Consequences: P1 (two ACs), P1.5 (CON-INT-002 errors), P3.1 (API document, catalog, adapter, concurrency note, registry count), P3.2 (upload error placement) and P4 (two backend cases, one frontend case) change additively. No id is renumbered or removed. Non-breaking.
traces      : REQ-INT-006, REQ-INT-009, REQ-INT-011, AC-INT-075, AC-INT-076

<<<END ADR>>>

<<<ADR: ADR-INT-026.md>>>
# ADR-INT-026 — Analysis-gate round 1 clarifications: same-type uploads passed through, background-poll failure keeps the shown Check, an invalid decision code tested, REQ-INT-057 worded without a modal, the P0 read-façade row annotated, and the partial-supersession status note
Status      : ACCEPTED
Stage       : P1        Module: INT        Version: v1
Context     : The round-1 analysis-gate review of INT v1 raised six MINOR findings (G3–G8). G3: module-registry's AUTO-DECISION row "INT adds no read façade" was never annotated as superseded by ADR-INT-020. G4: RULE-INT-002's invalid-decision-code half had no case. G5: REQ-INT-057 used the modal "may". G6: SCR-INT-002 did not say what one failed background poll does to a Check already on screen. G7: nothing said what happens when the employee uploads a second document of the same type before confirming. G8: ADR-INT-001, ADR-INT-017 and ADR-INT-018 remain ACCEPTED or RESOLVED-IN-DIALOGUE although later ADRs supersede some of their numbered points.
Decision    : (1) G7. Document Access accepts several uploads of one document type for a Check. A correction is a new upload, and the earlier one is never changed (REQ-DOC-023, AC-DOC-025). INT passes every such upload through as its own handover and never removes, replaces or picks among same-type uploads. Both are listed (REQ-INT-063), and which uploads the Check reads is Document Access's concern (CON-DOC-004). New REQ-INT-065 and AC-INT-077 state this. The upload screen (SCR-INT-003) marks a document type that already has an upload in its choice list and still offers it. (2) G6. The first read of a Check that fails shows the screen's error state with a retry action. A later background poll that fails keeps the last Check read successfully on screen and shows a non-blocking notice ("Refresh failed; retrying"). The next interval retries, and a successful read removes the notice. New REQ-INT-066 and AC-INT-078 state this. P3.2 records it in SCR-INT-002's States row and in the frontend plan's report data hook, and P4 adds a frontend case. (3) G4. New AC-INT-079 under REQ-INT-034 covers an employeeDecision outside {APPROVED, REJECTED}, and P4 adds a backend case. (4) G5. REQ-INT-057 is reworded to "operations published in the REST API document for every host system". The meaning does not change. (5) G3. The P0 AUTO-DECISION row is annotated with its supersession by ADR-INT-020. (6) G8 is a factory schema gap, not an INT defect, and is recorded here as a note only. The ADR status enum (shared/GOVERNANCE-CORE.md: ACCEPTED | BLOCKED | SUPERSEDED) cannot express partial supersession. The points superseded so far are ADR-INT-001's no-read part (by ADR-INT-020), ADR-INT-011 (5)'s current-version part (by ADR-INT-020), ADR-INT-017 (1) (by ADR-INT-020) and ADR-INT-018 (1), (2), (3) and (7) (by ADR-INT-021). ADR-INT-018 (4), (5) and (6) stay binding. The ADRs are not rewritten, and their Status lines stay as they are until the factory adds a "Superseded (partial)" field.
Alternatives rejected: for G7, "the latest upload of a type wins". INT would then decide which document the Check reads, which is Document Access's and the Check Engine's job. For G6, replacing the report with an error screen on one dropped poll; a transient network error would hide a valid report.
Consequences: P0 (one annotation), P1 (REQ-INT-065, REQ-INT-066, AC-INT-077, AC-INT-078, AC-INT-079, REQ-INT-057 wording), P3.2 (SCR-INT-002 and SCR-INT-003 states, frontend plan) and P4 (new cases) change additively. Non-breaking.
traces      : REQ-INT-034, REQ-INT-055, REQ-INT-057, REQ-INT-063, REQ-INT-065, REQ-INT-066, AC-INT-077, AC-INT-078, AC-INT-079

<<<END ADR>>>

<<<ADR: ADR-INT-027.md>>>
# ADR-INT-027 — DOC v1 delivered: the INT → DOC edge remains valid, with DOC v1's additions (CON-DOC-006 and the two upload refusals DOC-409-CHECK-ENDED and DOC-422-UPLOAD-LIMIT-REACHED) now consumed by INT; resolves the XM event INT>DOC
Status      : ACCEPTED
Stage       : P4        Module: INT        Version: v1
Context     : The factory raised the XM resolution event INT>DOC because Document Access (DOC) v1 was delivered and tagged (doc-v1) after INT designed against it. INT depends on DOC over the platform edge INT depends_on DOC (ADR-INT-016) and cites these DOC contract items: CON-DOC-001 (document read status and unreadable reason, read in the report), CON-DOC-002 (fetch mode, read in the report), CON-DOC-003 `handOverUpload(checkId, serviceCode, versionNumber, documentType, fileName, fileContent)` with its refusals, and CON-DOC-006 `listUploadedDocuments(checkId)`. DOC v1, compared with what INT first designed against, added two things. (a) CON-DOC-006, the listing operation, which INT already binds through API-INT-007 (ADR-INT-023). (b) Two further refusals of CON-DOC-003: "Check already ended" (DOC-409-CHECK-ENDED, ADR-DOC-015) and "maximum uploads per Check reached" (DOC-422-UPLOAD-LIMIT-REACHED, ADR-DOC-016). INT now passes both through (ADR-INT-025, analysis-gate round 1 finding G1). CON-DOC-004 and CON-DOC-005 are CHK's operations and are not consumed by INT.
Decision    : (1) Verified against DOC's delivered contract (analysis/modules/DOC/P1_5/contract-doc.md). CON-DOC-003's signature is unchanged from what INT's DocumentAccessPort adapter calls, and its six refusals are now all in INT's contract (CON-INT-002), API document (API-INT-002), error catalog (23 rows, DOC pass-through 6), adapter note and test cases (TC-INT-096, TC-INT-097, TC-INT-102). CON-DOC-006 is bound unchanged (ADR-INT-023, TC-INT-095). CON-DOC-001 and CON-DOC-002 carry the same closed code lists INT's report screens show. (2) The INT → DOC edge therefore remains valid. The only INT changes the delivery called for are the additive ones of ADR-INT-025. No plan is re-derived and no id is renumbered. (3) The event INT>DOC is answered with this ADR. A later DOC version that changes CON-DOC-001, CON-DOC-002, CON-DOC-003 or CON-DOC-006 raises a new event and is re-checked then.
Alternatives rejected: re-running INT's P1 … P4 against the delivered DOC. Only the two refusals were missing, and ADR-INT-025 added them additively, so a re-run would only reproduce the same artifacts.
Consequences: INT's XM event INT>DOC is resolved, and INT's plans keep their DOC bindings together with the ADR-INT-025 additions. Non-breaking.
traces      : REQ-INT-006, REQ-INT-009, REQ-INT-017, REQ-INT-063, AC-INT-075, AC-INT-076

<<<END ADR>>>

<<<ADR: ADR-INT-028.md>>>
# ADR-INT-028 — REG v1 delivered: every Service Registry item INT cites (CON-REG-002, CON-REG-004, CON-REG-009, CON-REG-012) is still honoured as INT uses it; INT already reads the required document types through CON-REG-009 with the Check's pinned version (ADR-REG-020); REQ-INT-016's stale source citation of CON-REG-010 is corrected; resolves the XM event XM-INT-001
Status      : ACCEPTED
Stage       : P1        Module: INT        Version: v1
Context     : The factory raised the XM resolution event XM-INT-001 (the entity-level edge INT → REG · ENT-REG-002, SOFT-READ, CONTRACTED on CON-REG-002 — ADR-INT-016) because the Service Registry (REG) v1 was delivered and tagged reg-v1 after INT designed against it. REG changed during its gate-analysis revise rounds: CON-REG-008 and CON-REG-010 gained `available` (ADR-REG-016); CON-REG-001 gained the canonical service-code form (ADR-REG-017); CON-REG-009's answer was widened additively (serviceCode, versionNumber, inputName, query detail, document source) and the version-pinning rule was stated — every read of service configuration inside a Check uses CON-REG-009 with the Check's pinned version, never CON-REG-007 (ADR-REG-020); CON-REG-005 gained RULE-REG-025 (ADR-REG-021). INT cites: CON-REG-002 (a recorded serviceCode + versionNumber never disappears — XM-INT-001's contract_ref), CON-REG-004 (the documentType values of a version), CON-REG-009 `getServicePackageVersion(serviceCode, versionNumber)` → `requiredDocumentTypes`, errors not found (API-INT-008, CON-INT-008, REQ-INT-064, `RegVersionDocumentsAdapter` on interface `ServiceRegistry`), and CON-REG-012 `getApprovalApi(serviceCode, versionNumber)` → {approvalEnabled, approvalApi when enabled}, errors not found (API-INT-004, `RegApprovalDefinitionAdapter` on the separate interface `ApprovalApiRegistry`, DBF-INT-009, DBF-INT-010). One stale line remained: REQ-INT-016's Source cited CON-REG-010 (the current-version summary), left from before ADR-INT-020 (5) moved the upload choices to the Check's own version.
Decision    : (1) Verified against REG's delivered contract (analysis/modules/REG/P1_5/contract-reg.md, identical to REG's _state/current-contract.md) and REG's backend plan (service layer of `ServiceRegistry` / `ApprovalApiRegistry`). CON-REG-002 keeps its promise (immutable, never deleted, resolvable through CON-REG-009). CON-REG-004's documentType values are unchanged. CON-REG-009 keeps `requiredDocumentTypes` and its not-found error (`VersionNotFoundException`) with the same signature; the added fields are additive and INT ignores them. CON-REG-012 keeps the same signature, fields, error and the Employee-Decision-only restriction INT's adapter honours. Nothing INT relies on was removed, renamed or changed in meaning. (2) ADR-REG-020: INT's only in-Check read of service configuration is API-INT-008, which already reads CON-REG-009 with the service code and version number the Report Store recorded for the Check (CON-RPT-003) — the pinned version (ADR-INT-020 (1), (5)); INT never calls CON-REG-007. CON-REG-012 is likewise read by the Check's own version. INT is compliant without a change of behaviour. (3) `available` (ADR-REG-016) is on CON-REG-008 and CON-REG-010, which INT does not call (its frontend binds only INT's own reads — ADR-INT-021); availability for a new Check is enforced by the Check Engine (CHK-422-SERVICE-NOT-AVAILABLE, passed through on API-INT-001), and a running Check's pinned version stays resolvable after a withdrawal (CON-REG-009 notes, ADR-REG-003), so INT surfaces no `available` flag. CON-REG-013 lists INT as a caller, but INT does not call it; that is REG's description and adds no INT obligation. (4) REQ-INT-016's Source is corrected from CON-REG-010 to CON-REG-009 (the Check's pinned version — ADR-INT-020, ADR-REG-020), matching REQ-INT-064, CON-INT-008 and API-INT-008. The edit is a provenance correction only: no statement, AC, API, code or test changes, and no id is added or renumbered. (5) The XM-INT-001 edge, its CONTRACTED state on CON-REG-002 and the CROSS-MOD block remain valid; the event XM-INT-001 is answered with this ADR. A later REG version that changes CON-REG-002, CON-REG-004, CON-REG-009 or CON-REG-012 raises a new event and is re-checked then.
Alternatives rejected: adding an `available` field to API-INT-008 — CON-REG-009 does not carry it and no INT requirement needs it; re-running INT's P1.5 … P4 — nothing they cite changed.
Consequences: INT's XM event XM-INT-001 is resolved; P1 changes one Source line; P1.5 … P4 keep their REG bindings unchanged. Non-breaking.
traces      : REQ-INT-016, REQ-INT-020, REQ-INT-025, REQ-INT-028, REQ-INT-030, REQ-INT-064, ENT-REG-002

<<<END ADR>>>

<<<ADR: ADR-INT-029.md>>>
# ADR-INT-029 — RPT v1 delivered: the Report Store contract items INT cites (CON-RPT-001 … CON-RPT-004, CON-RPT-006) are honoured as INT uses them, so the INT → RPT edge and every plan bound to it remain valid; resolves the XM event INT>RPT
Status      : ACCEPTED
Stage       : P4        Module: INT        Version: v1
Context     : The factory raised the XM resolution event INT>RPT because the Report Store (RPT) v1 was delivered and tagged rpt-v1 after INT designed against it. INT depends on RPT over the platform edge INT depends_on RPT (ADR-INT-016) and cites: CON-RPT-001 (the Check identifier, held by value as NUMBER(19)), CON-RPT-002 (Employee Decision, closed: APPROVED, REJECTED), CON-RPT-003 `readCheck(checkId)` (API-INT-005, and the status / service code / version / request number reads before an upload, a decision and API-INT-008), CON-RPT-004 `listChecksOfRequest(serviceCode, requestNumber)` (API-INT-006) and CON-RPT-006 `recordDecision(checkId, employeeDecision, decidedBy, approvalApiExecuted)` (API-INT-004), through `RptCheckRecordAdapter` on RPT's in-process interface `ReportStore`. CON-RPT-005 (decision agreement) is not consumed by INT (ADR-INT-020 (7)).
Decision    : (1) Verified against RPT's delivered contract (analysis/modules/RPT/P1_5/contract-rpt.md, identical to RPT's _state/current-contract.md) and RPT's backend plan (`ReportStore` service layer, error catalog). CON-RPT-003's answer carries every field of INT's CheckReportResponse with the same names and nullability; CON-RPT-004 carries {total, checks [checkId, status, overallStatus, startedAt, endedAt, employeeDecision]}, at most 100, newest first, exactly as ChecksOfRequestResponse relays it; CON-RPT-006 keeps its signature, its answer {checkId, employeeDecision, decidedBy, decidedAt, approvalApiExecuted} and its five refusals. CON-RPT-001 and CON-RPT-002 are unchanged. (2) The RPT codes INT passes through or raises — RPT-404-CHECK-NOT-FOUND, RPT-400-REQUEST-KEYS-MISSING, RPT-400-DECISION-INCOMPLETE, RPT-409-CHECK-NOT-COMPLETED, RPT-409-DECISION-ALREADY-RECORDED, RPT-422-APPROVAL-FLAG-ON-REJECTION — exist in RPT with the same English messages INT's error catalog carries. RPT's third RULE-RPT-013 wording (approvalApiExecuted absent) cannot arise from INT, which always sends the flag, so INT's catalog row stays as written. (3) Nothing INT relies on was removed, renamed or changed in meaning; the INT → RPT edge remains valid, no INT artifact changes and no plan is re-derived. The event INT>RPT is answered with this ADR. A later RPT version that changes CON-RPT-001 … CON-RPT-004 or CON-RPT-006 raises a new event and is re-checked then.
Alternatives rejected: re-running INT's P1 … P4 against the delivered RPT — nothing INT cites changed, so a re-run would only reproduce the same artifacts.
Consequences: INT's XM event INT>RPT is resolved; INT's plans keep their RPT bindings unchanged. Non-breaking.
traces      : REQ-INT-025, REQ-INT-026, REQ-INT-027, REQ-INT-061, REQ-INT-062, REQ-INT-064

<<<END ADR>>>
